\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|Z_{\text{HC}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(G_{X},\lambda_{X}}}\right)-Z\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathds{V},R,\lambda}}\right)}}\right|\leq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathrm{e}^{\frac{\varepsilon_{\text{D}}}{3}}-1}}\right)Z\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathds{V},R,\lambda}}\right)

for such point sets
X
.
Now, note that
\mathrm{e}^{\frac{\varepsilon_{\text{D}}}{3}}\leq\mathrm{e}^{\varepsilon_{\text{D}}}
and
2-\mathrm{e}^{\frac{\varepsilon_{\text{D}}}{3}}\geq\mathrm{e}^{-\varepsilon_{\text{D}}}
for
\varepsilon_{\text{D}}\in(0,1]
.
Therefore, we have

\mathrm{e}^{-\varepsilon_{\text{D}}}Z\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathds{V},R,\lambda}}\right)\leq Z_{\text{HC}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(G_{X},\lambda_{X}}}\right)\leq\mathrm{e}^{\varepsilon_{\text{D}}}Z\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathds{V},R,\lambda}}\right),

which concludes the proof.
∎

Although
Corollary

8.5
does not directly yield additional algorithmic results beyond what was already proved in
Section

6
, it shows an interesting relationship between the hard-core model on random geometric graph structures and continuous hard-constraint point process.
An immediate consequence of
Corollary

8.5
is that the hard-core partition functions of uniformly random geometric graphs on
\mathds{V}=[0,\ell)^{d}
with edge connection threshold
2r
for appropriately chosen fugacities concentrates around the partition function of a hard-sphere model with particle radius
r
on
\mathds{V}
as the number of vertices is increased.
We believe that this connection between hard-core models on random geometric graphs and the continuous hard-sphere model is of its own interest.

8.2
Concentration without star-convexity

All results considered so far used the assumption that the region
\mathds{V}
is star-convex.
This is essentially due to
Lemma

4.5
, which bounds the error introduced by mapping each point according to an allocation
\Phi
.
However, it turns out that a slightly weaker version of the concentration result holds without the assumption of star-convexity.

The core idea is to argue that
Z\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathds{V},R,\lambda}}\right)
is not significantly larger than
Z_{\Phi}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathds{V},R,\lambda}}\right)
, instead of arguing that they are close in general as done in
Lemma

4.5
.
This argument does not require star-convexity.
By randomizing the set of points, we obtain sufficient conditions for
Z_{\text{HC}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(G_{X},\lambda_{X}}}\right)
to concentrate from below.
The following general lemma then establishes that the partition function of the hard-core representation also concentrates from above.

\modifiedmarkov

Proof.

Let
A
denote the event that
X\geq(1-\varepsilon)\mathrm{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{X}}}\right]
and set
Y=\mathds{1}_{A}(X-(1-\varepsilon)\mathrm{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{X}}}\right])
.
Using the fact that
X
is non-negative and
\mathrm{Pr}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{A}}}\right]\geq 1-\delta
we obtain

\displaystyle\mathrm{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{Y}}}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathrm{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\mathds{1}_{A}X}}}\right]-(1-\varepsilon)\mathrm{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{X}}}\right]\mathrm{Pr}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{A}}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathrm{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{X}}}\right]-(1-\delta)(1-\varepsilon)\mathrm{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{X}}}\right]

\displaystyle=(\delta+\varepsilon-\delta\varepsilon)\mathrm{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{X}}}\right]

Further, observe that
Y
is non-negative and that
X\geq(1+c\varepsilon)\mathrm{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{X}}}\right]
if and only if
Y\geq(c+1)\varepsilon\mathrm{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{X}}}\right]
.
By using Markov’s inequality, we get

\displaystyle\mathrm{Pr}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{X\geq(1+c\varepsilon)\mathrm{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{X}}}\right]}}}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Pr}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{Y\geq(c+1)\varepsilon\mathrm{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{X}}}\right]}}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\mathrm{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{Y}}}\right]}{(c+1)\varepsilon\mathrm{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{X}}}\right]}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{(\delta+\varepsilon-\delta\varepsilon)\mathrm{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{X}}}\right]}{(c+1)\varepsilon\mathrm{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{X}}}\right]}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{c+1}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1+\frac{\delta(1-\varepsilon)}{\varepsilon}}}\right),

which concludes the proof.
∎

Next, given a suitable allocation
\Phi
, we show that
Z_{\Phi}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathds{V},R,\lambda}}\right)
is not significantly smaller than
Z\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathds{V},R,\lambda}}\right)
.

Lemma 8.6
.

Let
(\mathds{V},R,\lambda)
be a hard-constraint point process with
q\in\mathds{N}_{\geq 1}
particle types.
Further, set
R_{\max}=\max_{i,j\in[q]}\{R\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(i,j}}\right)\}
, and set
\lambda_{\max}=\max_{i\in[q]}\lambda\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(i}}\right)
.
Let
\delta\in\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[0,1}}\right]
and
\varepsilon\in\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[0,\frac{1}{2}}}\right]
and let
X\subseteq\mathds{V}
such that there is a
\delta
-
\varepsilon
-allocation
\Phi
for
X
.
Then

\sum_{k\in\mathds{N}}\sum_{\tau\colon[k]\to[q]}\int_{\mathds{V}^{k}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(w^{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(R,\lambda}}\right)}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\bm{x},\tau}}\right)-w^{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(R,\lambda}}\right)}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\Phi\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\bm{x}}}\right),\tau}}\right)}}\right)\,\text{d}\nu^{d\times k}\leq\varepsilon q\lambda_{\max}^{2}\nu\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathds{V}}}\right)\nu\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathds{B}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(R_{\max}+1}}\right)}}\right)Z\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathds{V},R,\lambda}}\right).

Proof.

By definition, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{k\in\mathds{N}}\sum_{\tau\colon[k]\to[q]}\int_{\mathds{V}^{k}}

\displaystyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(w^{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(R,\lambda}}\right)}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\bm{x},\tau}}\right)-w^{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(R,\lambda}}\right)}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\Phi\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\bm{x}}}\right),\tau}}\right)}}\right)\,\text{d}\nu^{d\times k}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k\in\mathds{N}}\frac{1}{k!}\sum_{\tau\colon[k]\to[q]}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\prod_{i\in[k]}\lambda\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\tau\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(i}}\right)}}\right)}}\right)\int_{\mathds{V}^{k}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(D_{\tau}^{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(R}}\right)}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\bm{x}}}\right)-D_{\tau}^{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(R}}\right)}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\Phi\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\bm{x}}}\right)}}\right)}}\right)\,\text{d}\nu^{d\times k}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k\geq 2}\sum_{\tau\colon[k]\to[q]}\frac{1}{k!}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\prod_{i\in[k]}\lambda\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\tau\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(i}}\right)}}\right)}}\right)\int_{\mathds{V}^{k}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(D_{\tau}^{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(R}}\right)}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\bm{x}}}\right)-D_{\tau}^{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(R}}\right)}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\Phi\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\bm{x}}}\right)}}\right)}}\right)\,\text{d}\nu^{d\times k}.

(18)

We proceed by bounding

\int_{\mathds{V}^{k}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(D_{\tau}^{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(R}}\right)}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\bm{x}}}\right)-D_{\tau}^{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(R}}\right)}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\Phi\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\bm{x}}}\right)}}\right)}}\right)\,\text{d}\nu^{d\times k}

from above.
Set

L^{(R)}_{\tau,\Phi}=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\bm{x}\in\mathds{V}^{k}\mid D_{\tau}^{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(R}}\right)}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\bm{x}}}\right)=1\text{ and }D_{\tau}^{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(R}}\right)}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\Phi\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\bm{x}}}\right)}}\right)=0}}\right\}

and observe that

\int_{\mathds{V}^{k}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(D_{\tau}^{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(R}}\right)}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\bm{x}}}\right)-D_{\tau}^{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(R}}\right)}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\Phi\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\bm{x}}}\right)}}\right)}}\right)\,\text{d}\nu^{d\times k}\leq\nu\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(L^{(R)}_{\tau,\Phi}}}\right).

Now, note that for all
\bm{x}=(x_{1}\dots,x_{k})\in\mathds{V}^{k}
it holds that
D_{\tau}^{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(R}}\right)}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\Phi\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\bm{x}}}\right)}}\right)=0
if and only if there are
i,j\in[k]
with
i\neq j
such that
d\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\Phi\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(x_{i}}}\right),\Phi\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(x_{j}}}\right)}}\right)<R\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\tau\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(i}}\right),\tau\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(j}}\right)}}\right)
.
Let

L^{(R)}_{\tau,\Phi}(i,j)=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\bm{x}\in\mathds{V}^{k}\mid D_{\tau}^{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(R}}\right)}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\bm{x}}}\right)=1\text{ and }d\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\Phi\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(x_{i}}}\right),\Phi\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(x_{j}}}\right)}}\right)<R\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\tau\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(i}}\right),\tau\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(j}}\right)}}\right)}}\right\}

and observe that

\nu\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(L^{(R)}_{\tau,\Phi}}}\right)\leq\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i,j\in[k]:\\
i<j\end{subarray}}\nu\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(L^{(R)}_{\tau,\Phi}(i,j)}}\right).

Thus, we obtain

\int_{\mathds{V}^{k}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(D_{\tau}^{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(R}}\right)}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\bm{x}}}\right)-D_{\tau}^{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(R}}\right)}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\Phi\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\bm{x}}}\right)}}\right)}}\right)\,\text{d}\nu^{d\times k}\leq\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i,j\in[k]:\\
i<j\end{subarray}}\nu\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(L^{(R)}_{\tau,\Phi}(i,j)}}\right).

Next, we upper bound
\nu\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(L^{(R)}_{\tau,\Phi}(i,j)}}\right)
for each
i,j\in[k]
with
i<j
.
To this end, we first introduce some additional notation.
For a finite set
S\subset\mathds{N}
and
i\in S
let
\text{ord}_{S}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(i}}\right)
denote the order of
i
in
S
.
That is,
\text{ord}_{S}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(i}}\right)\colon S\to[\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|S}}\right|]
with
\text{ord}_{S}(i)=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\{j\in S\mid j\leq i\}}}\right|
.
Let
k\geq 2
,
\bm{x}=(x_{1},\dots,x_{k})\in\mathds{V}^{k}
and
\tau\colon[k]\to[q]
.
For any
S\subseteq[k]
let
\bm{x}_{-S}\in\mathds{V}^{k-\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|S}}\right|}
denote
\bm{x}
after removing all components index by
S
.
Further, define
\tau_{-S}:[k-\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|S}}\right|]\to[q]
where for each
i\in[k]\setminus S
we set
\tau_{-S}(\text{ord}_{[k]\setminus S}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(i}}\right))=\tau\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(i}}\right)
.
Informally speaking,
\bm{x}_{-S}
and
\tau_{-S}
represent the configuration that results from
\bm{x}
and
\tau
after removing the particles index by the set
S
.

With this additional notation, we proceed with upper bounding
\nu\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(L^{(R)}_{\tau,\Phi}(i,j)}}\right)
.
Recall that
\Phi
is a
\delta
-
\varepsilon
-allocation, which implies
d\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(x,\Phi\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(x}}\right)}}\right)\leq\varepsilon
for all
x\in\mathds{V}
.
We obtain

\displaystyle\nu\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(L^{(R)}_{\tau,\Phi}(i,j)}}\right)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathds{V}^{k}}D_{\tau}^{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(R}}\right)}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\bm{x}}}\right)\mathds{1}_{d\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\Phi\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(x_{i}}}\right),\Phi\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(x_{j}}}\right)}}\right)<R\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\tau\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(i}}\right),\tau\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(j}}\right)}}\right)}\,\text{d}\nu^{d\times k}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathds{V}^{k}}D_{\tau_{-\{i,j\}}}^{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(R}}\right)}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\bm{x}_{-\{i,j\}}}}\right)\mathds{1}_{d\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(x_{i},x_{j}}}\right)\geq R\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\tau\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(i}}\right),\tau\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(j}}\right)}}\right)\text{ and }d\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\Phi\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(x_{i}}}\right),\Phi\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(x_{j}}}\right)}}\right)<R\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\tau\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(i}}\right),\tau\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(j}}\right)}}\right)}\,\text{d}\nu^{d\times k}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathds{V}^{k}}D_{\tau_{-\{i,j\}}}^{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(R}}\right)}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\bm{x}_{-\{i,j\}}}}\right)\mathds{1}_{R\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\tau\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(i}}\right),\tau\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(j}}\right)}}\right)\leq d\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(x_{i},x_{j}}}\right)<R\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\tau\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(i}}\right),\tau\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(j}}\right)}}\right)+2\varepsilon}\,\text{d}\nu^{d\times k}

\displaystyle\leq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\nu\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathds{B}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(R\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\tau\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(i}}\right),\tau\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(j}}\right)}}\right)+2\varepsilon}}\right)}}\right)-\nu\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathds{B}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(R\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\tau\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(i}}\right),\tau\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(j}}\right)}}\right)}}\right)}}\right)}}\right)\nu\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathds{V}}}\right)\int_{\mathds{V}^{k-2}}D_{\tau_{-\{i,j\}}}^{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(R}}\right)}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\bm{x}}}\right)\text{d}\nu^{d\times(k-2)}.

Further, observe that for
\varepsilon\leq\frac{1}{2}
it holds that

\displaystyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\nu\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathds{B}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(R\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\tau\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(i}}\right),\tau\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(j}}\right)}}\right)+2\varepsilon}}\right)}}\right)-\nu\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathds{B}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(R\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\tau\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(i}}\right),\tau\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(j}}\right)}}\right)}}\right)}}\right)}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\nu\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathds{B}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1}}\right)}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left((R_{\max}+2\varepsilon)^{d}-R_{\max}^{d}}}\right)

\displaystyle=\nu\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathds{B}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1}}\right)}}\right)\sum_{i=1}^{d}\binom{d}{i}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(2\varepsilon}}\right)^{i}R_{\max}^{d-i}

\displaystyle=2\varepsilon\nu\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathds{B}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1}}\right)}}\right)\sum_{i=1}^{d}\binom{d}{i}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(2\varepsilon}}\right)^{i-1}R_{\max}^{d-i}

\displaystyle\leq 2\varepsilon\nu\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathds{B}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1}}\right)}}\right)(R_{\max}+1)^{d}

\displaystyle=2\varepsilon\nu\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathds{B}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(R_{\max}+1}}\right)}}\right).

Thus, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathds{V}^{k}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(D_{\tau}^{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(R}}\right)}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\bm{x}}}\right)-D_{\tau}^{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(R}}\right)}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\Phi\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\bm{x}}}\right)}}\right)}}\right)\,\text{d}\nu^{d\times k}

\displaystyle\leq 2\varepsilon\nu\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathds{B}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(R_{\max}+1}}\right)}}\right)\nu\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathds{V}}}\right)\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i,j\in[k]:\\
i<j\end{subarray}}\int_{\mathds{V}^{k-2}}D_{\tau_{-\{i,j\}}}^{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(R}}\right)}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\bm{x}}}\right)\text{d}\nu^{d\times(k-2)}.

Substituting this back into
equation

18
and observing that

\displaystyle 2\varepsilon\nu\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathds{B}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(R_{\max}+1}}\right)}}\right)\nu\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathds{V}}}\right)\frac{1}{k!}

\displaystyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\prod_{l\in[k]}\lambda\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\tau\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(l}}\right)}}\right)}}\right)\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i,j\in[k]:\\
i<j\end{subarray}}\int_{\mathds{V}^{k-2}}D_{\tau_{-\{i,j\}}}^{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(R}}\right)}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\bm{x}}}\right)\text{d}\nu^{d\times(k-2)}

\displaystyle=2\varepsilon\nu\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathds{B}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(R_{\max}+1}}\right)}}\right)\nu\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathds{V}}}\right)\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i,j\in[k]:\\
i<j\end{subarray}}\int_{\mathds{V}^{k-2}}\frac{1}{k!}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\prod_{l\in[k]}\lambda\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\tau\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(l}}\right)}}\right)}}\right)D_{\tau_{-\{i,j\}}}^{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(R}}\right)}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\bm{x}}}\right)\text{d}\nu^{d\times(k-2)}

\displaystyle\leq 2\varepsilon\frac{\lambda_{\max}^{2}}{k(k-1)}\nu\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathds{B}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(R_{\max}+1}}\right)}}\right)\nu\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathds{V}}}\right)\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i,j\in[k]:\\
i<j\end{subarray}}\int_{\mathds{V}^{k-2}}\frac{1}{(k-2)!}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}l\in[k]:\\
l\notin\{i,j\}\end{subarray}}\lambda\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\tau\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(l}}\right)}}\right)}}\right)D_{\tau_{-\{i,j\}}}^{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(R}}\right)}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\bm{x}}}\right)\text{d}\nu^{d\times(k-2)}

\displaystyle=2\varepsilon\frac{\lambda_{\max}^{2}}{k(k-1)}\nu\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathds{B}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(R_{\max}+1}}\right)}}\right)\nu\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathds{V}}}\right)\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i,j\in[k]:\\
i<j\end{subarray}}\int_{\mathds{V}^{k-2}}w^{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(R,\lambda}}\right)}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\bm{x},\tau_{-\{i,j\}}}}\right)\text{d}\nu^{d\times(k-2)}

gives

\displaystyle\sum_{k\in\mathds{N}}\sum_{\tau\colon[k]\to[q]}\int_{\mathds{V}^{k}}

\displaystyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(w^{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(R,\lambda}}\right)}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\bm{x},\tau}}\right)-w^{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(R,\lambda}}\right)}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\Phi\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\bm{x}}}\right),\tau}}\right)}}\right)\,\text{d}\nu^{d\times k}

\displaystyle\leq 2\varepsilon\frac{\lambda_{\max}^{2}}{k(k-1)}\nu\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathds{B}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(R_{\max}+1}}\right)}}\right)\nu\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathds{V}}}\right)\sum_{k\geq 2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i,j\in[k]:\\
i<j\end{subarray}}\sum_{\tau\colon[k]\to[q]}\int_{\mathds{V}^{k-2}}w^{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(R,\lambda}}\right)}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\bm{x},\tau_{-\{i,j\}}}}\right)\text{d}\nu^{d\times(k-2)}

\displaystyle=2\varepsilon q^{2}\frac{\lambda_{\max}^{2}}{k(k-1)}\nu\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathds{B}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(R_{\max}+1}}\right)}}\right)\nu\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathds{V}}}\right)\sum_{k\geq 2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i,j\in[k]:\\
i<j\end{subarray}}\sum_{\tau\colon[k-2]\to[q]}\int_{\mathds{V}^{k-2}}w^{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(R,\lambda}}\right)}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\bm{x},\tau}}\right)\text{d}\nu^{d\times(k-2)}

\displaystyle=\varepsilon q^{2}\lambda_{\max}^{2}\nu\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathds{B}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(R_{\max}+1}}\right)}}\right)\nu\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathds{V}}}\right)\sum_{k\geq 2}\sum_{\tau\colon[k-2]\to[q]}\int_{\mathds{V}^{k-2}}w^{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(R,\lambda}}\right)}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\bm{x},\tau}}\right)\text{d}\nu^{d\times(k-2)}

\displaystyle=\varepsilon q^{2}\lambda_{\max}^{2}\nu\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathds{B}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(R_{\max}+1}}\right)}}\right)\nu\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathds{V}}}\right)\sum_{k\in\mathds{N}}\sum_{\tau\colon[k]\to[q]}\int_{\mathds{V}^{k}}w^{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(R,\lambda}}\right)}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\bm{x},\tau}}\right)\text{d}\nu^{d\times k}

\displaystyle=\varepsilon q\lambda_{\max}^{2}\nu\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathds{V}}}\right)\nu\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathds{B}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(R_{\max}+1}}\right)}}\right)Z\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathds{V},R,\lambda}}\right),

which concludes the proof.
∎

Combining
Lemma

8.6
and
Lemma

4.6
yields that
Z_{\text{HC}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(G_{X},\lambda_{X}}}\right)
also is not much smaller than
Z\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathds{V},R,\lambda}}\right)
.

Lemma 8.7
.

Let
(\mathds{V},R,\lambda)
be a hard-constraint point process with
q\in\mathds{N}_{\geq 1}
particle types.
For all
\varepsilon_{\text{D}}\in\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(0,1}}\right]
there are
\varepsilon,\delta\in\upTheta\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({\varepsilon_{\text{D}}\nu\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathds{V}}}\right)^{-1}}}}\right)
such that, for all finite
X\subset\mathds{V}
that have a
\delta
-
\varepsilon
-allocation and satisfy
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|X}}\right|\geq 64q\lambda_{\max}^{2}\nu\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathds{V}}}\right)^{2}\varepsilon_{\text{D}}^{-1}
, it holds that

\displaystyle Z_{\text{HC}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(G_{X},\lambda_{X}}}\right)\geq(1-\varepsilon_{\text{D}})Z\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathds{V},R,\lambda}}\right),

where
(G_{X},\lambda_{X})
is the hard-core representation of
(\mathds{V},R,\lambda)
based on
X
.

Proof.

Set
R_{\max}=\max_{i,j\in[q]}R\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(i,j}}\right)
and
\lambda_{\max}=\max_{i\in[q]}\lambda\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(i}}\right)
.
Further, set

\varepsilon=\frac{\varepsilon_{\text{D}}}{2q\lambda_{\max}^{2}\nu\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathds{B}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(R_{\max}+1}}\right)}}\right)\nu\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathds{V}}}\right)}\in\upTheta\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({\varepsilon_{\text{D}}\nu\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathds{V}}}\right)^{-1}}}}\right)

and

\delta=\frac{\varepsilon_{\text{D}}}{16q\lambda_{\max}\nu\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathds{V}}}\right)}\in\upTheta\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({\varepsilon_{\text{D}}\nu\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathds{V}}}\right)^{-1}}}}\right).

For
\nu\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathds{V}}}\right)
sufficiently large we have
\varepsilon\leq\frac{1}{2}
and thus, by
Lemma

8.6
, we obtain

\displaystyle Z\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathds{V},R,\lambda}}\right)-Z_{\Phi}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathds{V},R,\lambda}}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k\in\mathds{N}}\sum_{\tau\colon[k]\to[q]}\int_{\mathds{V}^{k}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(w^{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(R,\lambda}}\right)}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\bm{x},\tau}}\right)-w^{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(R,\lambda}}\right)}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\Phi\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\bm{x}}}\right),\tau}}\right)}}\right)\,\text{d}\nu^{d\times k}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\varepsilon_{\text{D}}}{2}Z\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathds{V},R,\lambda}}\right),

which implies

\displaystyle Z_{\Phi}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathds{V},R,\lambda}}\right)\geq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1-\frac{\varepsilon_{\text{D}}}{2}}}\right)Z\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathds{V},R,\lambda}}\right).

(19)

Further, note that
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|X}}\right|\geq 64q\lambda_{\max}^{2}\nu\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathds{V}}}\right)^{2}\varepsilon_{\text{D}}^{-1}\geq 4\lambda_{\max}\nu\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathds{V}}}\right)
.
Thus, by
Lemmas

4.7
and
4.6
, we have

\displaystyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|Z_{\Phi}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathds{V},R,\lambda}}\right)-Z_{\text{HC}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(G_{X},\lambda_{X}}}\right)}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k\in\mathds{N}}\sum_{\tau\colon[k]\to[q]}\int_{\mathds{V}^{k}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|w^{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(R,\lambda}}\right)}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\Phi\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\bm{x}}}\right),\tau}}\right)-w^{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(G_{X},\lambda_{X}}}\right)}_{\Phi}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\bm{x},\tau}}\right)}}\right|\,\text{d}\nu^{d\times k}

\displaystyle\leq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathrm{e}^{\frac{\varepsilon_{\text{D}}}{4}}-1}}\right)Z_{\Phi}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathds{V},R,\lambda}}\right).

As
\varepsilon_{\text{D}}\leq 2
it holds that

\displaystyle Z_{\text{HC}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(G_{X},\lambda_{X}}}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(2-\mathrm{e}^{\frac{\varepsilon_{\text{D}}}{4}}}}\right)Z_{\Phi}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathds{V},R,\lambda}}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1-\frac{\varepsilon_{\text{D}}}{2}}}\right)Z_{\Phi}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathds{V},R,\lambda}}\right).

Combining this with
equation

19
finally gives us

\displaystyle Z_{\text{HC}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(G_{X},\lambda_{X}}}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1-\frac{\varepsilon_{\text{D}}}{2}}}\right)Z_{\Phi}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathds{V},R,\lambda}}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1-\frac{\varepsilon_{\text{D}}}{2}}}\right)^{2}Z\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathds{V},R,\lambda}}\right)

\displaystyle\geq(1-\varepsilon_{\text{D}})Z\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathds{V},R,\lambda}}\right),

which proves the claim.
∎

By randomizing the considered set of points, we turn
Lemma

8.7
into a bound on how strong
Z_{\text{HC}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(G_{X},\lambda_{X}}}\right)
concentrates from below.
Combining this with
Section

1.5
then gives two-sided concentration.
However, note that
Section

1.5
only works for concentration around the expected value, but
Lemma

8.7
is given in terms of
Z\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathds{V},R,\lambda}}\right)
.
The following lemma will solves this problem by relating both quantities.

Lemma 8.8
.

Let
(\mathds{V},R,\lambda)
be a hard-constraint point process with
q\in\mathds{N}_{\geq 1}
particle types.
For all
n\geq 1
it holds that

\mathrm{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{Z_{\text{HC}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(G_{X},\lambda_{X}}}\right)}}}\right]\leq Z\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathds{V},R,\lambda}}\right),

where the expectation is taken over
X\subset\mathds{V}
with
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|X}}\right|=n
drawn uniformly at random.

Proof.

We start by rewriting
Z_{\text{HC}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(G_{X},\lambda_{X}}}\right)
as

\displaystyle Z_{\text{HC}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(G_{X},\lambda_{X}}}\right)=1+\sum_{k=1}^{n}\frac{\nu\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathds{V}}}\right)^{k}}{n^{k}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(k_{1},\dots,k_{q})\in\mathds{N}^{q}:\\
k_{1}+\dots+k_{q}=k\end{subarray}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\prod_{i\in[q]}\lambda\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(i}}\right)^{k_{i}}}}\right)\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}S_{1}\subseteq X:\\
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|S_{1}}}\right|=k_{1}\end{subarray}}\dots\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}S_{q}\subseteq X:\\
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|S_{q}}}\right|=k_{q}\end{subarray}}\mathds{1}_{\begin{subarray}{c}\forall i,j\in[q]\forall x_{1}\in S_{i},x_{2}\in S_{j}:\\
(i=j\text{ and }x_{1}=x_{2})\\
\text{ or }d\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(x_{1},x_{2}}}\right)\geq R\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(i,j}}\right)\end{subarray}}\penalty\ ,

where the condition
(i=j\text{ and }x_{1}=x_{2})
accounts for the fact that
G_{X}
does not contain self loops.
We proceed by rewriting this in terms of tuples of points from
X
.
To this end, for every
(k_{1},\dots,k_{q})\in\mathds{N}^{q}
define a canonical type assignment
\tau_{k_{1},\dots,k_{q}}:[k_{1}+\dots+k_{q}]\to[q]
with
\tau_{k_{1},\dots,k_{q}}(i)=j
such that
\sum_{l=1}^{j-1}k_{l}<i\leq\sum_{l=1}^{j}k_{l}
.
For any
k\in\mathds{N}
let
X^{k}_{\neq}=\{(x_{1},\dots,x_{k})\mid\forall i,j\in[k]\colon i\neq j\text{ implies }x_{i}\neq x_{j}\}
.
Further, for
\bm{x}=(x_{1},\dots,x_{k_{1}})\in X^{k_{1}}
and
\bm{y}=(y_{1},\dots,y_{k_{2}})\in X^{k_{2}}
write
\bm{x}\bm{y}
for the concatenation
(x_{1},\dots,x_{k_{1}},y_{1},\dots,y_{k_{2}})\in X^{k_{1}+k_{2}}
of both tuples.
Observe that

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}S_{1}\subseteq X:\\
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|S_{1}}}\right|=k_{1}\end{subarray}}\dots\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}S_{q}\subseteq X:\\
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|S_{q}}}\right|=k_{q}\end{subarray}}\mathds{1}_{\begin{subarray}{c}\forall i,j\in[q]\forall x_{1}\in S_{i},x_{2}\in S_{j}:\\
(i=j\text{ and }x_{1}=x_{2})\\
\text{ or }d\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(x_{1},x_{2}}}\right)\geq R\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(i,j}}\right)\end{subarray}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{k_{1}!}\sum_{\bm{x_{1}}\in X^{k_{1}}_{\neq}}\dots\frac{1}{k_{q}!}\sum_{\bm{x_{q}}\in X^{k_{q}}_{\neq}}D_{\tau_{k_{1},\dots,k_{q}}}^{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(R}}\right)}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\bm{x_{1}}\dots\bm{x_{q}}}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\prod_{i\in[q]}\frac{1}{k_{i}!}}}\right)\sum_{\bm{x}\in X^{k}}D_{\tau_{k_{1},\dots,k_{q}}}^{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(R}}\right)}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\bm{x}}}\right).

Thus, we have

Z_{\text{HC}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(G_{X},\lambda_{X}}}\right)=1+\sum_{k=1}^{n}\frac{\nu\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathds{V}}}\right)^{k}}{n^{k}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(k_{1},\dots,k_{q})\in\mathds{N}^{q}:\\
k_{1}+\dots+k_{q}=k\end{subarray}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\prod_{i\in[q]}\lambda\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(i}}\right)^{k_{i}}}}\right)\cdot\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\prod_{i\in[q]}\frac{1}{k_{i}!}}}\right)\sum_{\bm{x}\in X^{k}}D_{\tau_{k_{1},\dots,k_{q}}}^{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(R}}\right)}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\bm{x}}}\right),

which, by monotonicity and linearity of expectation yields

\displaystyle\mathrm{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{Z_{\text{HC}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(G_{X},\lambda_{X}}}\right)}}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq 1+\sum_{k=1}^{n}\frac{\nu\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathds{V}}}\right)^{k}}{n^{k}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(k_{1},\dots,k_{q})\in\mathds{N}^{q}:\\
k_{1}+\dots+k_{q}=k\end{subarray}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\prod_{i\in[q]}\lambda\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(i}}\right)^{k_{i}}}}\right)\cdot\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\prod_{i\in[q]}\frac{1}{k_{i}!}}}\right)\mathrm{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\sum_{\bm{x}\in X^{k}}D_{\tau_{k_{1},\dots,k_{q}}}^{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(R}}\right)}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\bm{x}}}\right)}}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq 1+\sum_{k\in\mathds{N}_{\geq 1}}\frac{\nu\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathds{V}}}\right)^{k}}{n^{k}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(k_{1},\dots,k_{q})\in\mathds{N}^{q}:\\
k_{1}+\dots+k_{q}=k\end{subarray}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\prod_{i\in[q]}\lambda\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(i}}\right)^{k_{i}}}}\right)\cdot\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\prod_{i\in[q]}\frac{1}{k_{i}!}}}\right)\mathrm{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\sum_{\bm{x}\in X^{k}}D_{\tau_{k_{1},\dots,k_{q}}}^{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(R}}\right)}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\bm{x}}}\right)}}}\right]

(20)

Let
x_{1},\dots,x_{n}
some arbitrary ordering of the points in
X
.
Now, note that

\sum_{\bm{x}\in X^{k}}D_{\tau_{k_{1},\dots,k_{q}}}^{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(R}}\right)}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\bm{x}}}\right)=\sum_{(i_{1},\dots,i_{k})\in[n]^{k}}D_{\tau_{k_{1},\dots,k_{q}}}^{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(R}}\right)}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(x_{i_{1}},\dots,x_{i_{k}}}}\right).

Thus, we get

\displaystyle\mathrm{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{\sum_{\bm{x}\in X^{k}}D_{\tau_{k_{1},\dots,k_{q}}}^{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(R}}\right)}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\bm{x}}}\right)}}}\right]=\sum_{(i_{1},\dots,i_{k})\in[n]^{k}}\mathrm{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{D_{\tau_{k_{1},\dots,k_{q}}}^{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(R}}\right)}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(x_{i_{1}},\dots,x_{i_{k}}}}\right)}}}\right]=\frac{n^{k}}{\nu\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathds{V}}}\right)^{k}}\int_{\mathds{V}^{k}}D_{\tau_{k_{1},\dots,k_{q}}}^{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(R}}\right)}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\bm{x}}}\right)\,\text{d}\nu^{d\times k}.

Substituting this back into
equation

20
and canceling some terms yields

\mathrm{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{Z_{\text{HC}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(G_{X},\lambda_{X}}}\right)}}}\right]\leq 1+\sum_{k\in\mathds{N}_{\geq 1}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(k_{1},\dots,k_{q})\in\mathds{N}^{q}:\\
k_{1}+\dots+k_{q}=k\end{subarray}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\prod_{i\in[q]}\lambda\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(i}}\right)^{k_{i}}}}\right)\cdot\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\prod_{i\in[q]}\frac{1}{k_{i}!}}}\right)\int_{\mathds{V}^{k}}D_{\tau_{k_{1},\dots,k_{q}}}^{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(R}}\right)}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\bm{x}}}\right)\,\text{d}\nu^{d\times k}.

Finally, observe that, for each
k\in\mathds{N}_{\geq 1}
and all
(k_{1},\dots,k_{q})\in\mathds{N}^{q}
with
k_{1}+\dots+k_{q}=k
, there a
\frac{k!}{\prod_{i\in[q]}k_{i}!}
type assignments
\tau\colon[k]\to[q]
that are equivalent to
\tau_{k_{1},\dots,k_{q}}
up to permutation of inputs (i.e.,
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\tau^{-1}(i)}}\right|=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\tau^{-1}_{k_{1},\dots,k_{q}}(i)}}\right|
for each
i\in[q]
).
Further, note that
\prod_{i\in[k]}\lambda\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\tau\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(i}}\right)}}\right)=\prod_{i\in[q]}\lambda\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(i}}\right)^{k_{i}}
and
\int_{\mathds{V}^{k}}D_{\tau}^{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(R}}\right)}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\bm{x}}}\right)\,\text{d}\nu^{d\times k}=\int_{\mathds{V}^{k}}D_{\tau_{k_{1},\dots,k_{q}}}^{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(R}}\right)}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\bm{x}}}\right)\,\text{d}\nu^{d\times k}
for each such
\tau
.
Thus, it holds that

\displaystyle\mathrm{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{Z_{\text{HC}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(G_{X},\lambda_{X}}}\right)}}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq 1+\sum_{k\in\mathds{N}_{\geq 1}}\frac{1}{k!}\sum_{\tau:[k]\to[q]}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\prod_{i\in[k]}\lambda\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\tau\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(i}}\right)}}\right)}}\right)\int_{\mathds{V}^{k}}D_{\tau}^{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(R}}\right)}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\bm{x}}}\right)\,\text{d}\nu^{d\times k}

\displaystyle=Z\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathds{V},R,\lambda}}\right),

proving the claim.
∎

We are now ready to state and prove our main theorem about concentration of random discretizations without star-convexity.

\concentrationnonstarconvex

Proof.

The main idea of the proof is to use
Lemma

8.1
and
Lemma

8.7
to argue that, for a uniformly drawn
X\subset\mathds{V}
that is sufficiently large, the hard-core partition function
Z_{\text{HC}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(G_{X},\lambda_{X}}}\right)
is with high probability not much smaller than the partition function
Z\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathds{V},R,\lambda}}\right)
of the continuous model.
Then we apply
Section

1.5
to derive a probabilistic upper bound on
Z_{\text{HC}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(G_{X},\lambda_{X}}}\right)

Let
c=\frac{p}{2}
,
\varepsilon_{\text{D}}^{\prime}=\frac{\varepsilon_{\text{D}}}{c}=\frac{p}{2}\varepsilon_{\text{D}}
and
p^{\prime}=\frac{\varepsilon_{\text{D}}}{4}p=\frac{\varepsilon_{\text{D}}^{\prime}}{2}
.
By
Lemma

8.7
, we know that there are
\varepsilon^{\prime},\delta^{\prime}\in\upTheta\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({\varepsilon_{\text{D}}^{\prime}\nu\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathds{V}}}\right)^{-1}}}}\right)=\upTheta\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({\varepsilon_{\text{D}}p\nu\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathds{V}}}\right)^{-1}}}}\right)
such that, for all
X\subset\mathds{V}
with
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|X}}\right|\geq 64q\lambda_{\max}^{2}\nu\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathds{V}}}\right)^{2}\varepsilon_{\text{D}}^{\prime-1}=128q\lambda_{\max}^{2}\nu\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathds{V}}}\right)^{2}\varepsilon_{\text{D}}^{-1}p^{-1}
that have an
\delta
-
\varepsilon
-allocation, it holds that

Z_{\text{HC}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(G_{X},\lambda_{X}}}\right)\geq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1-\varepsilon_{\text{D}}^{\prime}}}\right)Z\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathds{V},R,\lambda}}\right).

Next, note that we assume that there is a
\delta
-
\varepsilon
-partitioning of
\mathds{V}
of size
\mathrm{poly}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({\frac{\nu\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathds{V}}}\right)}{\varepsilon}}}}\right)
for some
\delta\in(0,1]
and all
\varepsilon\in\mathds{R}_{>0}
.
Thus, by
Lemma

8.1
we conclude that there is some

n^{\prime}_{\varepsilon_{\text{D}},p}\in\mathrm{poly}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({\frac{\nu\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathds{V}}}\right)}{\varepsilon^{\prime}\delta^{\prime}}\ln\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{1}{p^{\prime}}}}\right)}}}\right)=\mathrm{poly}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({\frac{\nu\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathds{V}}}\right)}{\varepsilon_{\text{D}}p}}}}\right)

such that for all
k\geq\mathds{V}
it hold that a uniformly random
X\subset\mathds{V}
with
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|X}}\right|=n
has a
\delta^{\prime}
-
\varepsilon^{\prime}
-allocation with probability at least
1-p^{\prime}
.

Setting

n_{\varepsilon_{\text{D}},p}=\max\{128q\lambda_{\max}^{2}\nu\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathds{V}}}\right)^{2}\varepsilon_{\text{D}}^{-1}p^{-1},n^{\prime}_{\varepsilon_{\text{D}},p}\}\in\mathrm{poly}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left({\frac{\nu\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathds{V}}}\right)}{\varepsilon_{\text{D}}p}}}}\right)

now ensures that for all
n\geq n_{\varepsilon_{\text{D}},p}

Z_{\text{HC}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(G_{X},\lambda_{X}}}\right)\geq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1-\varepsilon_{\text{D}}^{\prime}}}\right)Z\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathds{V},R,\lambda}}\right)

with probability at least
1-p^{\prime}
.
Further,
Lemma

8.8
implies that

\mathrm{Pr}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{Z_{\text{HC}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(G_{X},\lambda_{X}}}\right)<\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1-\varepsilon_{\text{D}}^{\prime}}}\right)\mathrm{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{Z_{\text{HC}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(G_{X},\lambda_{X}}}\right)}}}\right]}}}\right]\leq p^{\prime}.

By
Sections

1.5
and
8.8
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathrm{Pr}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{Z_{\text{HC}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(G_{X},\lambda_{X}}}\right)\geq(1+\varepsilon_{\text{D}})Z\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathds{V},R,\lambda}}\right)}}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathrm{Pr}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{Z_{\text{HC}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(G_{X},\lambda_{X}}}\right)\geq(1+\varepsilon_{\text{D}})\mathrm{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{Z_{\text{HC}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(G_{X},\lambda_{X}}}\right)}}}\right]}}}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Pr}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{Z_{\text{HC}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(G_{X},\lambda_{X}}}\right)\geq(1+c\varepsilon_{\text{D}}^{\prime})\mathrm{E}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[{Z_{\text{HC}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(G_{X},\lambda_{X}}}\right)}}}\right]}}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{c+1}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1+\frac{p^{\prime}(1-\varepsilon_{\text{D}}^{\prime})}{\varepsilon_{\text{D}}^{\prime}}}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{c+1}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1+\frac{1}{2}}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{3}{4}p.

Using the union bound on the failure probabilities, we obtain

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1-\varepsilon_{\text{D}}^{\prime}}}\right)Z\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathds{V},R,\lambda}}\right)\leq Z_{\text{HC}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(G_{X},\lambda_{X}}}\right)\leq(1+\varepsilon_{\text{D}})Z\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathds{V},R,\lambda}}\right)

with probability at least
1-p^{\prime}-\frac{3}{4}p=1-\frac{\varepsilon_{\text{D}}}{4}p-\frac{3}{4}p\geq 1-p
.
Finally, observing that
1+\varepsilon_{\text{D}}\leq\mathrm{e}^{\varepsilon_{\text{D}}}
and
1-\varepsilon_{\text{D}}^{\prime}=1-\frac{p}{2}\varepsilon_{\text{D}}\geq\mathrm{e}^{-\varepsilon_{\text{D}}}
concludes the proof.
∎

Remark 8.9
.

Note that the concentration result without star-convexity, given in
Section

1.5
, has a worse dependency on the desired error probability
p
than that obtained with star-convexity in
Section

1.5
.
This is due to the application of
Section

1.5
.
However, the dependency is still sufficient to construct a fully polynomial randomized approximation scheme (FPRAS) for
Z\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathds{V},R,\lambda}}\right)
.
